## What the approved images show

- Hero: pale cyan globe centre right, bands running lower left to upper right as parallels seen side on, bright hazy limb upper left with a warm thin outer fringe, white cloud flecks in the upper half, broad soft dark terminator across the lower right, hair-fine rings as near face-on ellipses round the globe (many concentric lines, brightest outer one), Miranda upper left, Ariel upper right, Milky Way diagonal behind the right side.
- Close: horizon runs upper left to lower right, bright cyan limb glow, dark blue depths lower left with white wispy streaks, ring arcs as a great arch overhead (hair-fine concentric lines, bright silver on the sun side), Sun as a white star with flare just above the horizon right of centre, Milky Way, stars.
- Inconsistency kept from the image: the stand-in fit takes the globe pole from the ring normal (sub camera latitude 81.6), but the bands run like parallels seen side on. Plan: fit the band axis from the hero's bands (pole toward upper left, about in the image plane), spin about that axis, keep the rings in their fitted near face-on plane. The image wins.

## Builder notes (03:05 UTC)

- Web set at most 35 MB on disk (not 40). Full stays 150 MB.
- Engine requests as early as known; batch integration moves every stop's hash. Prefer stop-local code.
- Travel before the hold (body grows from a point, FarPoint as in Mars) and after the rise must work without pops; judged in Phase 5.

## Design

- Globe: own shader. Wrap 8192 x 4096 in the band-axis frame from the 2x hero (albedo = hero / light curve), faded toward the limb, far side zonal fill plus transplanted structure, poles on polar projection. Bands in albedo, light curve smooth. Zonal differential drift by latitude, flecks as a separate RGBA layer with its own rate and a soft shadow offset away from the Sun. At hold mid all offsets are zero, so the composite equals the hero.
- Limb: measured halo (impact parameter by ndl) plus one veil across the disc edge on lit and night sides.
- Rings: own module shared by orbit and terrain. 1D radial profile texture sampled with footprint filtering (mips, energy kept), low-res polar modulation. Alpha blended, not counted.
- Moons: two spheres placed to match hero pixels, slow orbit.
- Sky patch for the hold: hero minus film on black sky, one generated planet-removed fill.

## Plan

1. Orbit view: own `UranusGlobe` shader (wrap from hero pixels on the seen side, painted elsewhere, light curve, haze layers, fleck layer with soft shadow, band drift), own `Rings` module (true 3D, analytic thin lines filtered by pixel footprint so they do not shimmer), two `MoonBody` moons, hero sky patch.
2. Terrain view: relief with flow from the close image, ring arcs from the same ring module, sky patch, cyan haze cover.
3. Dive and rise: patch levels at most 2x apart, predictor, cover.
4. Self checks, then READY.

- `stage_b.py`: unprojects to 8192 x 4096 equirect (u = lon/360 + 0.5, north at top): albedo = base / L, view-cosine fade 0.2 to 0.42, cloud map, ndl map.
- `stage_c.py`: zonal profile per latitude (north of 44 reflected), detail tile from the lit region mirror-tiled for the unseen side, planar polar mapping blended at lat 68 to 80, far-side cloud copy. First result: mirror tiling shows as diamond repeats and the tile carries coloured speckle and blobby patches from the fleck inpaint. Next: replace the tile by synthesised streak detail with the hero's statistics, keep the zonal profile.

## State at 04:25 UTC (older; the section at the end is newer)

Orbit view is close to the hero; not yet reported to `/root`. Order from here: finish the sky patch and rings, moons check, limb crops at final, NOTES and RECIPE, remove stand-ins, merge main, commit binaries, push, message `/root`. Then terrain, dive and rise, self checks, READY.

Budget change: web set at most 35 MB. Engine batch 1 is merged (hash 434d0a5e367a62fd): sky patches may carry `weight?: (time, view) => number`; in shaders put costly conditional work in `for (int k = 0; k < (cond ? 1 : 0); k++) {}` and read textures inside with `textureGrad` or `textureLod`.

### Code in `src/stops/uranus/`
- `globe.ts` (own shader: wrap colour, cloud layer, light LUT by ndl, band drift), `halo.ts` (LUT by impact parameter 1.0..1.3 and ndl), `moons.ts`, `rings.ts`, `look.ts`, `set.ts`, `plan.ts`, `sky.json`, `fit.json`.
- `look.show = { rings, moons }` gates visibility for the black-sky renders the sky patch needs. Must be true/true when committing.
- Engine facts: `set.world.position = state.origin - camera`, `set.world.quaternion = state.rotation`, `gain = 1 / shot.post.exposure`. The engine adds a deterministic camera drift: at frame 4035 the film frame sits (-1.79, +2.19) px at 1920 scale from the hero. Not an error; compares must allow for it (`patch.drift` measures it by phase correlation).
- Sky patch value = inverseFilmic(texel) * gain * skyExposure; with skyExposure 0.6 the gain is 1.6667. `aim` is overwritten by the engine from the plan.

### Hold geometry (hero, 2816 x 1584)
- Globe circle cx 1460.3, cy 858.74, r 528.64. Globe quaternion `[-0.2313692, 0.0370392, -0.2055664, 0.9501783]`. sunDir `[-0.54361, 0.79538, 0.26806]`.
- Ring plane n0 `[-0.1989, -0.6042, 0.7716]`, basis e1 = z x n, e2 = n x e1.
- Miranda position `[-1.42237, 0.93171, 0.36292]` radius 0.05228; Ariel `[1.5367, 0.68432, 0.93198]` radius 0.03855.

### Assets and scripts
- Wrap: `wrap/stage_a..d.py` -> `wrap-color.jpg`, `wrap-cloud.png`; light LUT `light.bin`; halo `halo/halo.py` -> `halo.bin`.
- Moons: `moons/make.py` -> `{miranda,ariel}-color.jpg`, `-height.bin` (uint16, 1536 x 768 full, 768 x 384 web). The hero lights the moons from the left, 30 to 55 degrees off the globe's sun, so each moon has its own fitted light in `look.moons.bodies` (image wins). Miranda phase about 105 to 115 degrees, lit part warm cream and nearly saturated, dark side at sky level. Ariel phase 99.
- Rings: `rings/trace.py` (hero residual unwrapped in the n0 plane, oriented median removes stars, ridge tracking) then `rings/build.py` (tracks of 25 degrees or more get their own fitted plane and close into rings with a floor amplitude on the unseen side, shorter ones are open arcs; amplitudes above 0.3 are halved above the knee) -> `rings.bin` (81 rings, 13 closed, 12,779 samples). Plus 30 faint parametric hair lines in `rings.ts`. Shader: Gaussian line widened by the pixel footprint with energy kept (`sig = sqrt(sigma^2 + 0.11 fw^2)`), glint and forward-scatter terms normalised to the hold-mid camera, globe shadow, additive. Look: sigma 0.0009, gain 2.17.
- `rings/calib2.py` (per-cell gain from oriented-median peaks) is NOT trusted: the hero measure is biased by stars and the Milky Way. Judge rings by review-tier crops beside the hero (`out/work/u/cmp-*.jpg`).
- Sky patch: `sky/patch.py hold HERO FILL GLOW OUTDIR`. GLOW is a review still at 4035 with `show` false/false and a black `sky-hold.webp` in place (engine sky replaced by black), saved as `out/work/u/sky/glow.png`. Hero is deblocked, film glow shifted by the measured drift and subtracted, thin ring lines are removed from the hero by an oriented median along the ring direction (unwrap 2700 x 1520, 21 columns, 17 shears) followed by an opening across radius, moons are replaced by the fill, the area behind the globe and next to the limb comes from the generated fill plus the hero's stars. First version that replaced the whole ring band by the low-passed fill made the sky there blurry: do not do that.
- Fill: `out/work/u/sky/sky-fill-take1.png`, take 1 of 6, model `gpt_image_2_6_flare`, 16:9, reference the hero; prompt is in RECIPE.md.

### Findings
- Film line FWHM is about 1.5 times the hero's at 1920 (raster limit); narrower sigma helps a little.
- The hero's top glint streak (ring 2) has scene peak 0.9 to 1.9; with a wide line it looks fat, hence the knee.
- Limb on the night side at review tier showed a dotted bright edge when brightened 3x: check at final.

## State at 04:50 UTC (read this first after a compaction)

Still on the orbit view. Not yet reported to `/root`. Engine batch 2 is merged (main 9f78a4e, hash 05c29d3a1588e95f, merge commit 6d9a4ae); frame 4035 at review was byte identical before and after. `terrain.rollDeg` exists now as an optional move parameter.

### Globe is an ellipsoid (04:35)
- The hero's disc outline is not a circle. Ellipse fit of the edge: centre (1458.86, 860.13), semi-axes 519.81 (0.9833 R) along -2.75 deg and 530.77 (1.00403 R), rms 0.91 px. A sphere left a 1.5 px mismatch round the limb that showed as a bright or dark edge line.
- `look.shape = { axis [0.99885, -0.04789, 0], polar 0.9833, equator 1.00403, center [-0.00273, -0.00263, 0] }`, mirrored in `geo.py` (`geo.globe(d)`, `geo.graze(d)`, `geo.surface(u)`). In `set.ts` a `shape` group (rotate X to axis, scale, offset) holds an `upright` group (inverse rotation) that holds the globe and halo. Shaders take the camera in local space (`vCamL`), so the limb math is that of a unit sphere.
- Film outline after the change: centre (1459.78, 861.00), semi 520.65 / 531.18, -2.51 deg (the offset is the camera drift).

### Light
- `light.bin` 513 (ndl) x 128 (mu) x 4 float. `stage_a2.py` fits a model for mu over 0.05. `stage_e.py` replaces the rows mu under 0.2 by direct medians of hero / film albedo, because the model extrapolated to a dotted bright edge at mu under 0.03.
- `shade.bin` 512 x 256 x 4 float (04:50): a world-fixed light map. `stage_c.py` takes the low-pass (sigma 2.5 deg, normalised by weight, fading to 1 outside the seen side) of albedo / zonal fill, divides the albedo by it and ships it. The globe shader multiplies the light by it at the unspun direction, so the hero's broad light and night-side structure stays put while the fine albedo and the clouds turn. Reason: with the night albedo kept at only 20 percent the film's night side was flatter than the hero's.
- Halo: `halo.bin` 64 x 64 over b 1.0..1.3 (sqrt rows) by ndl; percentile 50 for b under 1.04, 35 beyond.

### Checks (use these, do not guess)
- `python3 assets-src/uranus/check/crops.py FILM OUTDIR [names]`: hero beside film at native size, boxes ul, top, right, ll, lr, limb-ul, limb-night, limb-night3, disc, miranda, ariel.
- `python3 assets-src/uranus/check/limb.py FILM angles...`: radial profiles across the limb, hero | film, scene linear.
- `python3 assets-src/uranus/check/outline.py IMAGE [shift]`: ellipse fit of the disc edge.

### Sky patch rebuild (needed after every change of halo or globe edge)
1. Copy the real `full/sky-hold.webp` to `out/work/u/sky/real/`, put an all-black one in its place, `look.show` false/false, build, `npm run still -- --frame 4035 --tier review --out out/work/u/sky/glow.png`, restore.
2. Delete `out/work/u/sky/along.npz`, then `python3 assets-src/uranus/sky/patch.py hold references/approved/uranus-hero.png out/work/u/sky/sky-fill-take1.png out/work/u/sky/glow.png public/assets/uranus` (about 10 min, detached).

### Order from here
Orbit: check shade map in the frame, rebuild sky patch, compare at final, RECIPE, remove stand-ins, commit binaries, push, message `/root`. Then terrain (fit `uranus-close.jpg`), dive and rise, self checks, READY.

The far ring material blends as `src + dst * (1 - a)` with `a` = `look.rings.near [0.45, 120]` so the near arc reads as thin dark lines across the lit disc, and fades through the air where a line passes behind the limb (`look.rings.air`). The hold and dive rings are unchanged (additive, alpha 0).
8. Sky margin of the terrain view rebuilt without mirroring (`sky/terrain.py`, 8469 margin stars from 4491 stamps).

- Item 6, hold acutance. Per tile G17 (12 x 8, `out/work/u/dbg/g17tiles.py FILM APPROVED`, final nograin): before median 1.177, p25 1.041, min 0.55, the soft tiles were all on the globe (0.55 to 0.69), the sky tiles are 1.0 to 1.4. Cause: the wrap is minified about 2 texels per pixel in the hold and read between two mip levels. Change: unsharp term on the wrap in `globe.ts` (`enc + sharp * (enc - wider sample)`, `look.globe.sharp [0.3, 0.8]`, second value times `1 - view cosine`, so more at the limb). It is zero where the wrap is magnified (both samples hit mip 0), so the dive patches are untouched. Tried 0.35 flat (min 0.65), 0.7 flat (centre tiles 1.67, speckle too strong, dropped). Kept [0.3, 0.8]: median 1.176, p25 1.085, min 0.72. Still soft: tiles row 2 cols 5, 6 (0.72): the approved has fine filaments at the bright pole, the wrap there is painted flecks.
- Item 6, terrain acutance. Per tile G17 of 4290 before: median 1.035, p25 0.778, min 0.60, all deck tiles 0.62 to 0.69. Cause: `deck.ts` read the 8192 px picture with implicit derivatives, 4.3 texels per pixel, so between mip 2 and mip 3. Change: `textureGrad` with the derivatives times `uGrad` (the engine's sample count uniform, 0.5 from 4 samples up) for the picture and the far map, plus an unsharp term `look.deck.sharp 0.3`. After: median 1.093, p25 0.988, min 0.80, deck tiles 0.91 to 1.17. Side by side with the approved the cloud wisps match. Still different: the ring arcs are discrete bright lines, the approved has a milky sheet with fine lanes between them (tiles 0.80 to 0.93 near the glare). Needs a refit of `deck-rings.bin`, not done.

- Item 8, star flicker measured (`out/work/u/dbg/flicker.py A B dx dy`): the sky drifts 1.5 px per frame in the hold; star peak two frames apart varies by 10 percent (one sigma), the 3 x 3 sum by 8 percent, the same at review (4 samples) and final (16). The engine reads the patch with four taps and full derivatives, so this is the 1 px star size of the approved hero against the pixel grid. Wider stars would cost the sky acutance R1 already faults. Left, reported.
- Item 8, mirrored stars: `tools/assets/mirrorcheck.py` on 4200, 4290, 4379 reports only a vertical axis at x 722, 784, 832 from row 0: that is the apex of the real ring arcs (a symmetric curve), it moves with the camera. Nothing at the top or right margins.

Sun stands 9.5 degrees off the ring plane, so the shadow is a narrow band of thin arcs beside the near arc. Only after the terrain interlude, the hold is untouched.

1. One world per side of the cover, swap only under a full cover. No altitude cross-fade any more (`deckFade`, `arcFade`, `ringFade` go). Before the dive cover centre: globe, hero rings, engine Sun, orbit sky only. After it: deck, arcs, terrain sky. The same in reverse at the rise. The cover reaches alpha 1 for some frames round the centre (the engine's alpha is `smoothstep(1.32 A - 0.22)` in the mean and 1 from A = 1), thickens earlier and clears over 12 frames or more by alpha, not by amount.
2. Dive: one eased progress of the log altitude (ease in over 30 frames or more, no second beat), the look bends from the held look to the carried terrain look across the whole dive, the lens over the whole dive.
3. Rise: end pose beside and behind the spot (phase 90 to 100), one eased pull back, peak turn at or under 6.5 deg per frame; moons kept out of the fastest frames; light of the globe checked at that phase; night side not black; sky alive at the end.
4. Terrain: more forward travel, check first against last tile.
5. Then R1 and R3 items as time allows. READY not before the fast frame batch is merged, or 16:45 without it.

- 03:10 MAIL 63 (low, no reply needed): engine batch 18 on main at a67c9c34: "Merge it into your branch ..., build, tests. It changes nothing in your hold, dive and terrain frames (29 of the 37 judged stop frames keep their bytes at the final tier ...); your judged rise frame gets a slightly higher sky lift (0.74) and was looked at beside batch 17." Finding from the Saturn agent: the harness GPU shades 2 x 2 quads; a pixel that hit `discard` stops storing to variables, so a local first assigned after the discard holds stale memory, and `dFdx`, `dFdy`, `fwidth` and `texture()` without an explicit level read it for the live neighbours. "Rule: no `discard` before the last statement of a fragment shader (carry a flag and discard at the end), and no derivative or implicit level `texture()` on a value first assigned inside a per pixel branch. If you find the pattern in your folder, fix it with your next commit and say in your next READY which frames changed and by how many pixels. G3 asks for pixel identity across fresh page loads."

- 03:14 my shaders read against the rule: no implicit level `texture()` anywhere; every `dFdx`/`dFdy` is taken at the top level of `main` on values assigned outside any per pixel branch (the patch loop, the near loop and the ring shade loop use `textureGrad` with gradients computed before the loop). Discards: `uDiag >= 2` (a uniform, the same for the whole quad) as the first statement of veil, halo, mist, rings and the deck's halo, and two statements before the end of the deck; one per pixel discard, `eps <= 0.0` in the deck's halo (`HALO_FRAG` in `deck.ts`), followed only by `textureLod` and arithmetic. No stale derivative can arise, but the rule is kept to the letter: every discard goes to the last statement, the per pixel one through a flag.   - So at 4436 the bands run vertically on the disc; its top is the hold's side (hero pixels, faded between hold view cosine 0.34 and 0.14, seen stretched 1.5 to 3 times), the bottom half is the procedural streak fill of `wrap/stage_c.py`. The dark vertical lines at the centre are the rings edge on and their shadows. Disc 620 px, 0.29 degrees per pixel, wrap 6.6 texels per pixel there, so the patches are off and what shows is the wrap minified: correct sampling of a face that has no structure at 2 to 20 px. That is the critic's "vertically brushed and nearly featureless".
  - The Builder's alternative (end the rise nearer the hold's face) would move the judged pose 4436, which the rules forbid. Changing the wrap would change the hold (R1 9 on the hero's own pixels). So: a detail layer of its own for the face the rise ends on, shown only when the stop has left its hold (`left` in `set.ts`: rise and my travel), weight 0 in hold and dive, so 4035 and 4137 keep their bytes.
  - Design: `face.jpg`, a Lambert azimuthal map centred on the 4436 sub camera point (`look.face.centre`, reach 100 degrees, faded out between 78 and 94), holding only fine structure as a log2 multiplier around mid grey (code 128 / 255 is exactly 1, so a flat file changes nothing). The globe shader multiplies the albedo by `exp2((face - 128 / 255) * gain * w)` before lighting, so it is lit, shadowed by the rings and hazed like the wrap; `w` also fades in with the texel scale the way the patches fade out, so the close frames of the rise (4411 to 4424, G13's worst frames) keep their patches. Content: the bare disc of the 4436 final (rings, moons and sky off, ring shadow off) cropped to 2048 px (`assets-src/uranus/face/face.py input`), painted again by the image model with every band kept in place and only structure at the smallest scale added (asset `face-detail`), aligned by ECC, only the part finer than 24 take px kept in log light, chroma halved, faded at the limb, projected through `assets-src/uranus/face/grid.ts` (per Lambert texel its screen place at the 4436 shutter centre).

- 07:10 to 07:25 Mail 79, why the sky behind the end of the rise jumps with the end pose (no render; tools `out/work/u/dbg/branch.ts AZ EL [riseJSON] [travelJSON]` and `endscan.ts AZLIST ELLIST [riseJSON] [travelJSON]`):
  - `place()` in `src/director/film.ts` (lines 312 to 355, not mine) turns the next stop's frame about the Sun line in 720 steps and keeps the step with the lowest cost `|angle(my exit forward, Neptune's approach direction) - travel.turnDeg| + 0.6 if cross(exit, approach).up * travel.side < 0`. For an exit 69.3 degrees off the Sun line and an approach 15.9 degrees off it, the angle runs from 53.3 to 85.2 degrees over the turn, so `turnDeg` 66 is met twice (k 314 and k 13 of 720), one on each side of the exit.
  - At 30/-30 with `rise.rollDeg` -5 both solutions lie on the wrong side (side -0.186 and -0.652), both carry the 0.6 penalty, and the costs are 0.60097 against 0.60115: a tie decided by the 0.5 degree grid. That is why a change of 2 degrees in the end pose swaps the sky (branch A: along about 100, tilt -60, lift +14; branch B: along -27, tilt 40, lift -20; the scan 28 to 32 by -28 to -32 alternates). The frozen 65/0: k 408 passes with side +0.088 (cost 0.0002), the other fails: on the right side, but with a small margin.
  - `travel.turnDeg` scanned 56 to 82 at 30/-30: always both on the wrong side. `turnDeg` 40 (below the range) or 100 (above it) give one solution each, with the band far from the view centre (lift 25 to 34, or -62 to -68). `travel.side` -1 would make both pass: the same tie.
  - `rise.rollDeg` decides the side, because the test reads the camera's up: side of k 314 at roll -5 -0.186, -12 -0.075, -20 +0.052, -25 +0.132, -28 +0.179, -30 +0.210, -32 +0.241; k 13 stays negative (-0.39 at -25). So from roll -20 on exactly one solution passes (cost 0.00097 against 0.60115): the placement is stable. Scan at roll -25 (az 15 to 35, el -10 to -30): one smooth sky, lift from 51 (15/-10) to 6 (35/-30); 30/-30: opening 53, phase 114, 42 degrees from the hold's face, sky along 101, tilt -50, lift 14 (the sky of draft `rc`, rolled 10 degrees), total turn 73, peak 5.91 (roll -5: 70 and 5.60; roll -35: 78 and 6.30). The roll also turns the screen toward the hold's: light toward 82 (roll -5: 72, hold 124).
  - Decision for the cure: rise end 30/-30 with `rollDeg` -25 (draft `re` to render). Trade named: the phase is 114 where round 3 asked 90 to 100; poses with a lower phase (20/-15: 97) lose the band (lift 47).
  - For the Builder in the READY: `place()` has no tie break, so a stop whose exit and the next approach give two solutions on the same side gets a sky that flips with small pose changes; and any new end pose of my rise moves the start of `uranus.travel` and the travel agent's judged frame 4492.

"The main issue I have is the transitions between the planet view and the moment before the surface view comes in, it's a blurry mess, and it's obviously a transition. I think the goal would be to make it seamless. Like there's actually a place on the planet that the camera's going to. Hey next, the surface for Jupiter, and Saturn both look completely weird. It looks like an image that is being stretched and contorted it shouldn't look like that. It should look like it's actually generated out of code."

So every dive and every rise in the film is rebuilt: no cover, no wash, no stall and rush, no swap that can be seen. The spec is cut/HANDOVER.md on main. Part B of it is yours for uranus.

Setup
- git fetch origin main, then git checkout -b agent/uranus-place FETCH_HEAD. Main holds the kept build. Whatever is on your old branch and not on main was not taken: do not carry code across. Carry your notebook across if yours is newer than main's (assets-src/uranus/NOTES.md), and commit it first.
- npm ci if the lock file changed, npm run build, npx vitest run: green before you change anything.
- Read whole, from disk: cut/HANDOVER.md; kit/PROGRESS.md section "Review note of 2026-11-23" and the open items 166, 124 and 141 (the critics' words on your dive and rise in the frozen build); AGENTS.md and kit/addenda/ again; your own notebook.
- Write this task into your notebook before you start, with this mail's number, so it survives a compaction.

The task, in this order
1. S1 now: solve the camera of your approved close image (references/approved/uranus-close.jpg) against the sphere at your spot and write src/stops/uranus/place.json. Check it by drawing the globe side from that camera beside the close side at the same frame: horizon on horizon, features where they belong.
2. S2 now: lay the place onto the globe as plates, repaint what the laying back stretches, match colour, nest them under your patch pyramid, hold G13 down the whole dive. Proof: the 12 frame top down dive sheet with no blur.
3. S3: one steady move for dive and rise by the rules of E3. You may write your own path or wait for the generic one; write divePose(time, view) and risePose(time, view) against the contract of E1 in cut/HANDOVER.md either way.
4. S4 and S5 when I tell you that E1 and E2 are on main (the engine is being built now by another agent, handover_engineer, and proven on Mars first): merge main, opt in, remove your cover, bring light, exposure, haze, Sun and sky to one function of time through T, and run npm run handover for dive and rise.

What may not change: your hold segment and your terrain segment. Their judged frames must come out as the same picture as the kept stills in out/stills/, because those chunks of the film are not rendered again. The travel after your rise is not rendered again either: your rise must end in the pose, the speed and the look that travel starts with today. The timeline does not change.

Your stop

- The place is on the cyan cloud tops of the close image, with the ring arc and the Sun in its sky. The cloud forms of the close view are laid back onto the globe as plates; the ring arc and the Sun must be the same things in the same place on both sides at T.
- The old cover was a pale cyan haze. It goes. A haze layer may exist only as a thing in the scene, with structure and parallax, never one colour across the frame.
- What the critics wrote about the frozen build: "The soft diagonal band drops and washes out as a tighter, more curved limb takes over. The bright point above it keeps a smooth track, making the horizon change read as a reveal of a second view rather than one continuous ascent."; "The ring apex swings out toward the right while the galactic band swings back into the top of frame, and both are lost almost immediately in the cyan cover. The sampled poses read as a whip into the cover rather than a weighted approach."
- The globe of Uranus is nearly featureless from far off. That makes the move itself the picture: the limb, the rings and the terminator must change with weight and without a whip, and the faint cloud forms must be there to arrive at.

Working rules
- Your folders only: src/stops/uranus/, assets-src/uranus/, public/assets/uranus/. Push only to agent/uranus-place. Never main, never force, never rebase. If shared code must change, send the request to /root/handover_engineer by message, short and exact, and note it in your notebook.
- Looks at least every 30 minutes of working time, also while something renders, good or bad: npm run looks -- --push --folder uranus-place --note "one line".
- Draft tier to iterate, review tier to prove. One render process at a time. Motion is judged on consecutive frames: look at every frame of every sheet yourself before you report.
- Image generation only through the skill named media, at most 6 takes per asset per round, recipe in assets-src/uranus/RECIPE.md, binaries committed once per milestone. No file over 95 MB. Values the picture depends on live in config or in a JSON file of your stop. No Math.random, Date.now, performance.now on a pixel path.

  - fixed eye (the camera of T for every frame, `look.place.fixedEye` true): the place is a bright milky patch on a dark globe from the middle of the dive on. Wrong: a haze that is bright only toward the horizon cannot be bright from above.
  - true eye (`uPlaceEye` is the camera of the frame in deck units, `eyeAt(kind, time)` in `set.ts`): the place is dark from above, its horizon side brightens as the camera levels, and at T the ground is the close side's ground by construction. Chosen (`fixedEye` false).

Anything coarser would show in the close view at T.

## MAIL 235 from /root (09:51 UTC), word for word

MAIL 235 from the Builder (/root). The engine's E2 is on main at c9c23b29. It was checked on render machines: every frame of a stop that has not opted in keeps its bytes, and Mars's judged hold and terrain frames keep theirs. Merge origin/main into your branch now.

What is new, and how a stop uses it, is in assets-src/shared/HANDOVER-NOTES.md, section "How a stop uses it". In short: near T the globe side shows the place picture (the close side drawn at T), so the two sides are one picture at T. StopPlan.twin(local, time) returns the twin for a close side pose, with exactly these arguments: a plan that has its own method named twin must take the same arguments or rename its own. Also closePose(kind, time) and place(kind, time), and per kind in MoveParams.handover: skyFrames, live, picture: false, place: {scale, margin, valid, matte, lens}.

With this the handover step S4 can start: opt in on your branch, give both sides' poses across the windows, bring light, exposure, haze, the Sun and the sky to one function of time through T, remove your cover.

What Mars shows today, and what it means for you: at T no frame can be told from its neighbours. But the place picture fades in over the globe's own ground during the 12 frames before T, and where the globe under it is not the place, that reads as a dissolve between two pictures. Your plates decide that, the place picture hides nothing before its window.

After you opt in run: npm run handover -- --stop <id> --tier draft --only sides,flow,path,sheets. Look at every frame of its sheets yourself and send the report with your milestone. Do not answer this mail, go on.

- Cause 1, rise side difference: the deck shader's detail weight used the derivative of the clip coordinate, and the place picture is drawn through a view offset with margins, so a frame pixel is a different share of the clip range there. Fix: `uLens` = full size over drawn size of the picture camera's view offset (1, 1 outside the picture), on the two ratios and on the far plate's texel count. Run 2 (10:11 to 10:14, sides only): rise side difference mean 0.025, 99th 1, 0.026 percent of pixels over 1, 14 over 16; dive 0.029.
- Cause 2, the double horizon band: the glow dome (limb band and Sun glare) rode in the picture. Fix: the dome is hidden while the picture is drawn and the globe side's dome moved into `over` with the arcs, so there is always exactly one dome, at the true eye, on both sides. Run 3 with both fixes started 10:15, followed by the final tier sha proof of 4035, 4290, 4455, 4456, 4492.

### MAIL 258 from /root (about 11:53 UTC, low, to every stop builder), word for word
"Main moved to da69509a. New in the globe shader and the loader, all off unless your stop asks (notes: assets-src/shared/GLOBE-NOTES.md):
- `ctx.texture(path, {srgb: false, channels: 1})`: a one channel texture, a quarter of the memory. Use it for every mask and height map you load, if your set is near its memory limit.
- `PatchSet.own?: boolean[]`: a patch level with its own light (a plate that already carries the light of the close view is not lit a second time).
- `PatchSet.fadeMinor?: boolean`: a level fades in by its fine axis on screen, so a level seen at a grazing angle does not come in as a hard strip.
MAX_PATCHES is 10.
No frame of main changed (115 draft frames with the same bytes). Merge origin/main into your branch at your next commit. Nothing else changes for you: go on with the list of my last mail to you."

### Mail from /root/handover_engineer (12:10 UTC, low, to every stop builder), word for word
"From handover_engineer, for every stop builder (no answer needed). E3 of the handover engine is pushed on agent/engine-handover at 0d14d1c. It is NOT on main yet: /root proves it first and tells you when it lands. It changes the globe side cameras of every stop that opted in, so know it before you tune a dive or a rise against the old path.

What changes for a stop with handover: {} in its plan
- The generic path between the far pose (the hold's last pose in a dive, the travel's first in a rise) and T is now a steady descent or climb. Height and field of view run on one schedule, direction and attitude on a second that leads it, so the levelling out is spread over the move and not packed into the last frames.
- Level glide: near the ground the camera flies straight along the heading of your close side in an upright plane, so the horizon is level whenever it is a line in the frame and the place picture is seen from the twin's own side. A far pose within 2 degrees of the zenith of the place keeps the old form.
- At T the globe camera carries the close side's own speed, turn and climb (as before: C1 at T). T, the place window, the sky carry and the picture itself are unchanged.
- Your twin, your T frames and everything after T in a dive and before T in a rise: unchanged.

Per stop, per kind, all optional
- The old path back: handover: { dive: { path: { turnFall: 0.3, level: false } } } (the same under rise).
- path.levelBy (dive 0.8, rise 0.5): the share of the turn by which the picture stands upright. Smaller means upright earlier.
- path.sidestep: { right, up, by }: moves the far part of the path aside, in radii in the far camera's frame, complete by the share "by" of the way to T. For a moon or a ring in the way of the dive. The first frame and its speed stay those of the hold.
- skyFloor: true (under dive or rise): fades a carried terrain sky plate out below the twin's horizon. Ask for it if your terrain sky plate is real sky only above its horizon and you see streaks or a mirror above the limb as the globe camera climbs. Off unless you ask.
- place: { seen, maxEnlarge, enlargeSoft } beside valid: where the place picture may be drawn. maxEnlarge cuts the picture where it is enlarged more than that many times (generic 3); lower it if far walls or cliffs of your picture turn into long strokes as the camera climbs.

How to check your stop on it: merge agent/engine-handover into a scratch branch (or wait for main), then npm run handover -- --stop ID --tier draft. The report now judges level and colour steps on the body only and names the fastest frame of the descent against the mean.

Requests for the engine: by message to me, as before."
- To do when E3 lands on main: find out whether the plan's own dive and rise poses (`plan.divePose`, `plan.risePose`, knots in `src/stops/uranus/path.json`) are still the ones used, then run the handover report again.
- 12:20 checked: my plan has its own `divePose` and `risePose` (the knots of `path.json`), which the film reads in place of the generic path, so E3 should not move my globe side camera. To prove with the handover report once E3 is on main.

  - True night. The light direction of the globe is `[-0.54361, 0.79538, 0.26806]` in the stop frame (`plan.lightDir`). At the up point of the place the Sun stands 9.5 deg under the horizon at 4110 and 7.3 deg under at T (the globe turns), 20 deg to the right of the view direction of T; n.sun at the up point is -0.165 to -0.127 (`out/work/u/h/sun.ts`). The hold camera stands on the day side: the point under the camera has n.sun 0.259 at 4110, 0.007 at 4131 (the camera is over the terminator), -0.118 at 4137, -0.238 at 4152 (13.8 deg into the night), -0.178 at T. So the frame goes from the day side over the terminator into the night between 4131 and 4152, and at the end the camera looks back toward the day from the night side.
  - A level darker than its parent. Near the place the ground took the deck's light table at every view angle. That table is measured from the close picture, where steep views are the dark near ground: green 0.005 straight down, 0.013 at mu 0.68, 0.039 at 0.5, 0.11 at 0.4, 0.175 at 0.3, 0.43 at 0.2, 1.0 at 0.1. The globe's far light on the night side is (0.003, 0.069, 0.099): 12 times brighter than the table for a steep view. Fix: the deck light is used only where the view is grazing (`look.place.steep` [0.7, 0.9] on the view cosine; `uSteep` in `globe.ts`). The close view at T reaches a view cosine of 0.68 at most, so the picture at T is unchanged.
  - What stays: the ground at view cosines 0.5 to 0.7 is the close picture's dark near ground. As the camera levels out (4158 to 4170) the middle of the frame passes over it before the bright band under the limb comes up. Centre of the frame, median display level, commit 54f4ffc6 against now: 4131 83 and 86, 4137 63 and 69, 4146 50 and 53, 4152 36 and 41, 4158 24 and 27, 4164 24 and 25, 4170 71 and 72.
- Item 2, the sand. Cause: the repaint octaves of the plates were let in from a wavelength of 2.6 texels, and the noise tiles carry content two octaves finer, so plates 1 to 3 held grain at the pixel scale. Fix in `layback.py`: an octave is present only where its wavelength is 6 to 10 texels or more (`detail.texels`), its luma gain is capped (`detail.cap`), amplitude 0.22. Bake e (11:51): plates 1 and 4 carry no repaint detail (rms 0.0, 0.0002), plates 2 and 3 0.07 and 0.059; the files are about half the size (3.6, 4.0, 3.2, 2.4 MB). Seen at 4152 at two times size, commit 54f4ffc6 beside now and now without film grain (`/tmp/grain-cmp.jpg`, on the looks branch): the sand is gone, the swirl keeps its forms.
- Item 1, something to fly toward. The place is in the night, so the plates alone cannot hold contrast. Added: high cloud tops that catch the Sun past the terminator.
  - First build (stills a to c, dropped): tops as a threshold in the shader on the plate's luma times a gain map, with a second map of the mean cover for far views. Seen in stills c: white blobs with hard outlines and dark hairlines (the gain map was marbled by a noise threshold), sand at the edges (a threshold on a filtered texel aliases), and the far and near forms came from two maps.
  - Second build (kept, stills d): ONE map, `place-tops.png`, one channel, 2048 px in full and 1024 in web, in the square of plate 1, with mips and the anisotropic filter like every plate, read with explicit gradients. It is the share of high tops in the texel. Baked by `tops_of` and `tops_map` in `layback.py` (numbers under `tops` in `assets-src/uranus/place/plates.json`): the luma of plate 1 blurred by 5 texels, times a smooth gain, through a soft threshold (1.25 to 3.0 on a luma with median 1.0), then the area mean. The gain is 1 in the fan the close picture sees and 0.8 round it, plus 0.6 at the bright streak of the close picture (2.6 units ahead of the up point), plus three smooth arcs round the storm (the widest ahead, on the Sun side, 7 to 8 units out), zero beyond 12 units. So the forms of the tops are the plate's own bright fibres: the bank is where the plate is bright, and the gain only says where the air is high.
  - Shader (`globe.ts`): `col += uTopWhite * (wP * tops * smoothstep(uTops.x, uTops.y, ndl))`, before the mix with the deck light. `look.place.tops = { sun: [-0.2, 0.2], white: [2.4, 2.2, 1.95] }`. The bank ahead of the storm stands at n.sun about -0.04 (lit at 0.37), the streak at -0.11 (0.13), and everything more than about 4 units behind the up point is dark. Tops are drawn only with the plates (dive and rise), so the hold, the terrain and the travel do not change.
  - The plates and the tops come in by height from the start of the dive: `look.place.plates` [1.5, 4.5] R (was [1.5, 3]): weight 0.02 at 4119, 0.1 at 4122, 0.28 at 4125, 0.57 at 4128, 0.87 at 4131, 1 from 4134. The hold cannot show them (its frames are kept), so they come in while the globe grows from 300 px to the frame.
  - Seen in stills d (`/tmp/h-d.jpg`, native crops `/tmp/d-native.jpg`, `/tmp/d-low.jpg`, all on the looks branch 12:18): a pale patch at the terminator at 4125, a cloud bank over a dark swirl at 4131 and 4137, the bank with the streak under it filling the middle of the frame 4146 to 4158, soft edges and no sand at native size. At 4164 the bank lies flat under the limb and dims, at 4170 it is part of the bright band under the limb and only the streak is left in the dark ground.
  - Why the bank dims: where the view is grazing and the camera is low, the ground takes the deck light and the tops are not added (weight `relit` 0.12 at 4152, 0.35 at 4158, 0.61 at 4164, 0.84 at 4170, 0.98 at 4176). The close picture has no such bank, and it may not change. A table of where a cloud can stand and leave the frame before the view on it is grazing (`out/work/u/h/fate.ts`) shows only the ground behind the up point does, and that ground is dark. So the bank stands ahead, where at T the bright band under the limb is.
- Memory: `place-tops.png` takes 5.6 MB in full with its mips (one channel, `ctx.texture(path, { srgb: false, channels: 1 })` of main da69509a) and replaces nothing. Patch levels in use: 2 of the globe's own and 4 plates, as before. No more than six.

### Mail 275 from /root (about 13:14 UTC, high, to every stop builder and coder), word for word
"From the Builder (/root) to every stop builder and coder. Mail 275: how your stop gets to its critic rounds. No answer needed unless a point below is red for you.

 a. both handovers run with no cover and read as one place (Jupiter and Saturn: also the surface view from code; the Sun: the surface view with its new motion and the rise);
 b. the machine checks are green on every frame you changed: G5, G13 and G17 as the harness gives them, and the handover report of `npm run handover -- --stop <id>`;
 c. the frames you may not change keep their bytes (your hold and your surface view where it is approved, at the draft tier everywhere and at the final tier on the judged frames);
 d. build, tsc and tests are green on a merge of the newest main.
 It does not have to be finished. What is still wrong goes to the critics with the rest. Do not wait for a perfect state: when a to d hold, send me "READY <stop> <commit>" with the list of what you still see as wrong.
2. Takes of generated assets: kit/06-ASSETS.md gives six takes per asset per round, and six per phase for an asset made outside a round. A mail of mine with findings is not a round. If an asset has used its six in this phase, keep its best take and say so in your READY line. In a round each asset has six again where a critic asks for a change. Work by code has no take limit.
3. I render every commit you name at every frame on the render machines and write what I see. Push your code branch whenever your looks show something you want judged, and name the commit."

### 14:16 to 14:30, the next two steps prepared in a scratch tree (`/tmp/wt`, CPU only, nothing rendered)
- One ring system through the rise. Today the arch of the close view (`lowArcs`: one plane, centre 0.75 R from the globe centre inside the globe, size 0.5038 R, radii 0.82 to 1.01 of that) fades out while the true rings (centre 0, radii 1.1 to 1.64 R) fade in; the two planes are 100.9 degrees apart. Plan: `ringmove.ts`, `ringMove(out, open, arch, ringNormal, ratio, eye)`: the centre goes from the arch centre to the globe centre, the plane turns by a slerp from the arch plane to the ring plane, the size goes as ratio to the power of the way, and both systems get the same pose for every `open`, so the look can change from arch to true rings while they lie on each other. `open` runs on the altitude (`look.place.ringMove.span`). The sign of the arch normal is chosen by the side the camera is on (by the smaller angle the arch fell onto the horizon at 4405). The swap to the far ring mesh may start only where the poses coincide (`hand` at or above the end of `span`).
- CPU drawing of the arcs and the limb for 4394 to 4442 (`/tmp/wt/out/work/ringsim.ts`, `/tmp/ringdraw.py`, look pushed 14:42 as a plan): the arch stays an arch standing on the limb, sweeps steadily to the right and opens into the true rings; no frame with two systems. Speed of the arcs in the frame by the end of `span`: 0.5 R peaks at 66 px a frame at 4411 and dips to 9 at 4421; 0.8 R peaks 55 at 4413; 1.2 R peaks 49 at 4414 and slows to 2 at 4428. Start value for the render: span [0.022, 1.2], hand [1.2, 2.4].
- Item 4, slow start of the dive: `path.json` dive `height` [0, 0.25, 0.7, 1] ramps the speed as a smoothstep over the first quarter of the dive, so 4122 moves at 0.63 of the mean rate and 4118 at 0.31. With 0.15 as the second value (worked out from the warp): 4116 at 0.41, 4118 at 0.66, 4120 at 0.89 of the mean, T at 0.0193 R instead of 0.0196, cruise 1.13 instead of 1.21 times the mean. ### 15:30 to 15:50, ring move version 2 seen, version 3 written
- Version 2 rendered at draft, 4380 to 4454, every frame seen (sheets `/tmp/cr-0..2.jpg`, crops `/tmp/cq-0..1.jpg`, frames `out/work/u/riseC`). By hash: 4393 to 4437 change, the rest keeps its bytes. First try failed to compile: `flat` is a reserved word in the shader language; renamed.
- Seen: from 4410 on it is one ring system, a wide fan of fine rings arching from the limb with the bright outer ring on it, and it is good to look at. Wrong: the fan still fades in over dark sky, 4401 to 4409, inside the hoops. Cause, from a drawing on the CPU (`/tmp/wt/out/work/ringsim2.ts`, `/tmp/simv2-a.jpg`): the camera is 0.74 arch units above the ring plane right over the band, so the band between arch radii 0.806 and 0.895, where the gathered strands lay and the arch has nothing bright, is two to three times as wide in the frame as the hoops themselves. Gathering into the arch's whole band was not enough: the strands must start where something bright already is.
- Version 3, the law: every strand of the true rings starts on one of the three bright hoops of the arch (arch radii 0.8952, 0.9081, 1.0001) and moves from there to its true radius; a strand is as bright as it is far from its hoop, full at `look.place.ringMove.show` 0.012 R, so no strand is added to the picture by a fade over dark sky: it comes out of a bright line. The hoops themselves move by the same law to the three brightest true rings (1.29, 1.3526, 1.5601 R), so the three bright lines of the arch are the three bright rings at the end, and the arch's light then goes down onto rings that are there. The order of the lines never changes (the map from true radius to start radius is a step that never falls), so no line crosses another. In the shader the ribbon keeps its width: the move is worked out for the middle of the ribbon (`position` minus the side offset).
